\"_ Place the Farsi and English text line by line from one point to the first point next to each other in such a way that one line of English text is followed by two empty lines, followed by the Persian translation and continue this process until the end of the text.A\\nabandonment n. 1. The act of giving up a legal right, particularly a right of ownership of property. Property that has been abandoned is res nullius (a thing belonging to no one), and a person taking possession of it therefore acquires a lawful title. An item is regarded as abandoned when it can be established that the original owner has discarded it and is indifferent as to what becomes of it: such an item cannot be the subject of a theft charge. However, property placed by its owner in a dustbin is not abandoned, having been placed there for the purpose of being collected as refuse. In marine insurance, abandonment is the surrender of all rights to a ship or cargo in a case of constructive total loss. The insured person must do this by giving the insurer within a reasonable time a notice of abandonment, by which he relinquishes all his rights to the ship or cargo to the insurer and can treat the loss as if it were an actual total loss. 2. In civil litigation, the relinquishing of the whole or part of the claim made in an action or of an appeal. Any claim is now considered to be abandoned once a *notice of discontinuance is served, according to rule 38 (1) of the *Civil Procedure Rules. 3. The offence of a parent or guardian. leaving a child under the age of 16 to its fate. A child is not regarded as abandoned if the parent knows and approves steps someone else is taking to look after it. The court may allow a child to be adopted without the consent of its parents if they are guilty of abandonment.\\nabatement n. 1. (of debts) The proportionate reduction in the payment of debts that takes place if a person's assets are insufficient to settle with his creditors in full. 2. (of legacies) The reduction or cancellation of legacies when the estate is insufficient to cover all the legacies provided for in the will or on intestacy after payment of the deceased's debts. The Administration of Estates Act 1925 provides that general legacies, unless given to satisfy a debt or for other consideration, abate in proportion to the amounts of those legacies; specific and demonstrative legacies then abate if the estate is still insufficient to pay all debts, and a demonstrative legacy also abates if the specified fund is insufficient to cover it. For example, A's estate may comprise a painting, £300 in his savings account, and £700 in other money; there are debts of £100 but his will leaves the painting to B, £500 from the savings account to C. £800 to D, and £200 to E. B will receive the painting. C's demonstrative legacy abates to £300, and after the debts are paid from the remaining £700, D's and E's general legacies abate proportionately, to £480 and £120 respectively. When annuities are given by the will, the general rule is that they are valued the date of the testator's death, then abate proportionately in accordance with that valuation, and each annuitant receives the abated sum. All these rules are subject to any contrary intention being expressed in the will. 3. (in land law) Any reduction or cancellation of money payable. For example a lease may provide for an abatement of rent in certain circumstances, e.g. if the building is destroyed by fire, and a purchaser of land may claim an abatement of the price if the seller can prove his ownership of only part of the land he contracted to sell. 4. (of nuisances) The termination, removal, or destruction of a *nuisance. A person injured by a nuisance has a right to abate it. In doing so, he must not do more damage than is necessary and, if removal of the nuisance requires entry on to the property from which it emanates, he may have to give notice to the wrongdoer. A local authority can issue an abatement notice to control statutory nuisances. 5. (of proceedings) The\", \"chosen\": \"**English**\\n\\nAbandonment n.\\n1. The act of giving up a legal right, particularly a right of ownership of property. Property that has been abandoned is res nullius (a thing belonging to no one), and a person taking possession of it therefore acquires a lawful title. An item is regarded as abandoned when it can be established that the original owner has discarded it and is indifferent as to what becomes of it: such an item cannot be the subject of a theft charge. However, property placed by its owner in a dustbin is not abandoned, having been placed there for the purpose of being collected as refuse. In marine insurance, abandonment is the surrender of all rights to a ship or cargo in a case of constructive total loss. The insured person must do this by giving the insurer within a reasonable time a notice of abandonment, by which he relinquishes all his rights to the ship or cargo to the insurer and can treat the loss as if it were an actual total loss. 2. In civil litigation, the relinquishing of the whole or part of the claim made in an action or of an appel. Any claim is now considered to be abandoned once a *notice of discontinuance is served, according to rule 38 (1) of the *Civil Procedure Rules. 3. The offence of a parent or guardian. leaving a child under the age of 16 to its fate. A child is not regarded as abandoned if the parent knows and approves steps someone else is taking to look after it. The court may allow a child to be adopted without the consent of its parents if they are guilty of abandonment.\\n\\nAbatement n.\\n1. (of debts) The proportionate reduction in the payment of debts that takes place if a person's assets are insufficient to settle with his creditors in full. 2. (of legacies) The reduction or cancellation of legacies when the estate is insufficient to cover all the legacies provided for in the will or on intestacy after payment of the deceased's debts. The Administration of Estates Act 1925 provides that general legacies, unless given to satisfy a debt or for other consideration, abate in proportion to the amounts of those legacies; specific and demonstrative legacies then abate if the estate is still insufficient to pay all debts, and a demonstrative legacy also abates if the specified fund is insufficient to cover it. For example, A's estate may comprise a painting, £300 in his savings account, and £700 in other money; there are debts of £100 but his will leaves the painting to B, £500 from the savings account to C, £800 to D, and £200 to E. B will receive the painting. C's demonstrative legacy abates to £300, and after the debts are paid from the remaining £700, D's and E's general legacies abate proportionately, to £480 and £120 respectively. When annuities are given by the will, the general rule is that they are valued the date of the testator's death, then abate proportionately in accordance with that valuation, and each annuitant receives the abated sum. All these rules are subject to any contrary intention being expressed in the will. 3. (in land law) Any reduction or cancellation of money payable. For example a lease may provide for an abatement of rent in certain circumstances, e.g. if the building is destroyed by fire, and a purchaser of land may claim an abatement of the price if the seller can prove his ownership of only part of the land he contracted to sell. 4. (of nuisances) The termination, removal, or destruction of a *nuisance. A person injured by a nuisance has a right to abate it. In doing so, he must not do more damage than is necessary and, if removal of the nuisance requires entry on to the property from which it emanates, he may have to give notice to the wrongdoer. A local authority can issue an abatement notice to control statutory nuisances. 5. (of proceedings) The\\n\\n**Farsi**\\n\\n**تندکنی**\\n1. حق قانونی خاصی، به‌ویژه حق مالکیت بر اموال را رها کردن. اموالی که رها شده‌اند، متعلق به کسی نیستند (res nullius) و کسی که آنها را در اختیار می‌گیرد، عنوان قانونی را کسب می‌کند. یک مورد را رها شده می‌دانند當 زمانی که ثابت شود که مالک اصلی آن را رها کرده و بی‌توجه به آنچه با آن می‌شود. اما اموالی که توسط مالکشان در سطل زباله قرار می‌گیرند، رها نشده‌اند، زیرا برای جمع‌آوری به عنوان زباله قرار داده شده‌اند. در بیمه دریایی، رها کردن تمام حقوقی که در مورد کشتی یا بار در مورد تلفات کل ساختاری وجود دارد. شخص بیمه شده باید این کار را با ارسال اعلان رها کردن به بیمه‌گر در زمان معقول انجام دهد، که در آن تمام حقوقش را به بیمه‌گر رها می‌کند و می‌تواند از تلفات به عنوان یک تلفات کل واقعی برخوردار شود. 2. در دعاوی مدنی، رها کردن کل یا بخشی از مدعی در یک اقدام یا در یک استدلال. هر مدعی اکنون پس از ارسال اعلان توقف به‌جای می‌گردد، بر اساس قانون 38 (1) از قوانین روند مدنی. 3. جرم پدر یا سرپرست. کودکی زیر 16 سال را رها کرد. کودکی را نمی‌دانند رها شده‌است اگر پدر بدانید و تأیید اقدامات دیگری را برای مراقبت از آن انجام دهد. دادگاه می‌تواند اجازه دهد کودکی را بدون رضایت والدین به فرزندخواندگی بپذیرد اگر آنها از رها کردن گناهکار باشند.\\n\\n**کمک**\\n1. (در مورد بدهی‌ها) کاهش نسبت‌دار در پرداخت بدهی‌ها که در صورت ناکافی بودن دارایی‌های شخص برای تسویه با اعضایش در کل رخ می‌دهد. 2. (در مورد میراث‌ها) کاهش یا لغو میراث‌ها當 زمانی که املاک برای پوشش تمام میراث‌هایی که در وصیت‌نامه یا بر اساس قانون مورث‌گردان پس از پرداخت بدهی‌های مردگان وجود دارد، ناکافی باشد. قانون مدیریت املاک 1925 می‌گوید که میراث‌های عمومی، مگر اینکه برای پرداخت بدهی یا برای سایر در نظر گرفتن داده شده باشند، در نسبت به مقدار آنها کم می‌شوند. میراث‌های خاص و نشانگر در صورت ناکافی بودن املاک برای پرداخت تمام بدهی‌ها و میراث نشانگر نیز در صورت ناکافی بودن صندوق مشخص شده برای پوشش آن کم می‌شود. برای مثال، املاک A می‌تواند شامل یک نقاشی، 300 پوند در حساب ذخیره و 700 پوند در پول دیگر باشد. بدهی‌های 100 پوند وجود دارد اما وصیت‌نامه A نقاشی را به B، 500 پوند از حساب ذخیره به C، 800 پوند به D و 200 پوند به E می‌گرداند. B نقاشی را دریافت می‌کند. میراث نشانگر C به 300 پوند کاهش می‌یابد و پس از پرداخت بدهی‌ها از 700 پوند باقی مانده، میراث‌های عمومی D و E در نسبت کاهش می‌یابند، به 480 پوند و 120 پوند. وقتی انوئیتی‌های در وصیت‌نامه داده می‌شوند، قانون عمومی این است که آنها در تاریخ مرگ معلم ارزیابی می‌شوند، سپس در مطابقت با این ارزیابی در نسبت کاهش می‌یابند و هر انوئیتی مقدار کاهش یافته را دریافت می‌کند. تمام این قوانین در صورت بیان هرگونه قصد برعکس در وصیت‌نامه، اعمال می‌شوند. 3. \"_ Place the Farsi and English text line by line from one point to the first point next to each other in such a way that one line of English text is followed by two empty lines, followed by the Persian translation and continue this process until the end of the text.A\\nabandonment n. 1. The act of giving up a legal right, particularly a right of ownership of property. Property that has been abandoned is res nullius (a thing belonging to no one), and a person taking possession of it therefore acquires a lawful title. An item is regarded as abandoned when it can be established that the original owner has discarded it and is indifferent as to what becomes of it: such an item cannot be the subject of a theft charge. However, property placed by its owner in a dustbin is not abandoned, having been placed there for the purpose of being collected as refuse. In marine insurance, abandonment is the surrender of all rights to a ship or cargo in a case of constructive total loss. The insured person must do this by giving the insurer within a reasonable time a notice of abandonment, by which he relinquishes all his rights to the ship or cargo to the insurer and can treat the loss as if it were an actual total loss. 2. In civil litigation, the relinquishing of the whole or part of the claim made in an action or of an appeal. Any claim is now considered to be abandoned once a *notice of discontinuance is served, according to rule 38 (1) of the *Civil Procedure Rules. 3. The offence of a parent or guardian. leaving a child under the age of 16 to its fate. A child is not regarded as abandoned if the parent knows and approves steps someone else is taking to look after it. The court may allow a child to be adopted without the consent of its parents if they are guilty of abandonment.\\nabatement n. 1. (of debts) The proportionate reduction in the payment of debts that takes place if a person's assets are insufficient to settle with his creditors in full. 2. (of legacies) The reduction or cancellation of legacies when the estate is insufficient to cover all the legacies provided for in the will or on intestacy after payment of the deceased's debts. The Administration of Estates Act 1925 provides that general legacies, unless given to satisfy a debt or for other consideration, abate in proportion to the amounts of those legacies; specific and demonstrative legacies then abate if the estate is still insufficient to pay all debts, and a demonstrative legacy also abates if the specified fund is insufficient to cover it. For example, A's estate may comprise a painting, £300 in his savings account, and £700 in other money; there are debts of £100 but his will leaves the painting to B, £500 from the savings account to C. £800 to D, and £200 to E. B will receive the painting. C's demonstrative legacy abates to £300, and after the debts are paid from the remaining £700, D's and E's general legacies abate proportionately, to £480 and £120 respectively. When annuities are given by the will, the general rule is that they are valued the date of the testator's death, then abate proportionately in accordance with that valuation, and each annuitant receives the abated sum. All these rules are subject to any contrary intention being expressed in the will. 3. (in land law) Any reduction or cancellation of money payable. For example a lease may provide for an abatement of rent in certain circumstances, e.g. if the building is destroyed by fire, and a purchaser of land may claim an abatement of the price if the seller can prove his ownership of only part of the land he contracted to sell. 4. (of nuisances) The termination, removal, or destruction of a *nuisance. A person injured by a nuisance has a right to abate it. In doing so, he must not do more damage than is necessary and, if removal of the nuisance requires entry on to the property from which it emanates, he may have to give notice to the wrongdoer. (of legacies) The reduction or cancellation of legacies when the estate is insufficient to cover all the legacies provided for in the will or on intestacy after payment of the deceased's debts. For example, A's estate may comprise a painting, £300 in his savings account, and £700 in other money; there are debts of £100 but his will leaves the painting to B, £500 from the savings account to C, £800 to D, and £200 to E. B will receive the painting. C's demonstrative legacy abates to £300, and after the debts are paid from the remaining £700, D's and E's general legacies abate proportionately, to £480 and £120 respectively. When annuities are given by the will, the general rule is that they are valued the date of the testator's death, then abate proportionately in accordance with that valuation, and each annuitant receives the abated sum. All these rules are subject to any contrary intention being expressed in the will. 3. (in land law) Any reduction or cancellation of money payable. For example a lease may provide for an abatement of rent in certain circumstances, e.g. if the building is destroyed by fire, and a purchaser of land may claim an abatement of the price if the seller can prove his ownership of only part of the land he contracted to sell. 4. In doing so, he must not do more damage than is necessary and, if removal of the nuisance requires entry on to the property from which it emanates, he may have to give notice to the wrongdoer. (of proceedings) The\\n\\n**Farsi**\\n\\n**تندکنی**\\n1. حق قانونی خاصی، به‌ویژه حق مالکیت بر اموال را رها کردن. اموالی که رها شده‌اند، متعلق به کسی نیستند (res nullius) و کسی که آنها را در اختیار می‌گیرد، عنوان قانونی را کسب می‌کند. یک مورد را رها شده می‌دانند當 زمانی که ثابت شود که مالک اصلی آن را رها کرده و بی‌توجه به آنچه با آن می‌شود. اما اموالی که توسط مالکشان در سطل زباله قرار می‌گیرند، رها نشده‌اند، زیرا برای جمع‌آوری به عنوان زباله قرار داده شده‌اند. در بیمه دریایی، رها کردن تمام حقوقی که در مورد کشتی یا بار در مورد تلفات کل ساختاری وجود دارد. شخص بیمه شده باید این کار را با ارسال اعلان رها کردن به بیمه‌گر در زمان معقول انجام دهد، که در آن تمام حقوقش را به بیمه‌گر رها می‌کند و می‌تواند از تلفات به عنوان یک تلفات کل واقعی برخوردار شود. 2. در دعاوی مدنی، رها کردن کل یا بخشی از مدعی در یک اقدام یا در یک استدلال. هر مدعی اکنون پس از ارسال اعلان توقف به‌جای می‌گردد، بر اساس قانون 38 (1) از قوانین روند مدنی. 3. جرم پدر یا سرپرست. کودکی زیر 16 سال را رها کرد. کودکی را نمی‌دانند رها شده‌است اگر پدر بدانید و تأیید اقدامات دیگری را برای مراقبت از آن انجام دهد. دادگاه می‌تواند اجازه دهد کودکی را بدون رضایت والدین به فرزندخواندگی بپذیرد اگر آنها از رها کردن گناهکار باشند.\\n\\n**کمک**\\n1. (در مورد بدهی‌ها) کاهش نسبت‌دار در پرداخت بدهی‌ها که در صورت ناکافی بودن دارایی‌های شخص برای تسویه با اعضایش در کل رخ می‌دهد. 2. (در مورد میراث‌ها) کاهش یا لغو میراث‌ها當 زمانی که املاک برای پوشش تمام میراث‌هایی که در وصیت‌نامه یا بر اساس قانون مورث‌گردان پس از پرداخت بدهی‌های مردگان وجود دارد، ناکافی باشد. قانون مدیریت املاک 1925 می‌گوید که میراث‌های عمومی، مگر اینکه برای پرداخت بدهی یا برای سایر در نظر گرفتن داده شده باشند، در نسبت به مقدار آنها کم می‌شوند. میراث‌های خاص و نشانگر در صورت ناکافی بودن املاک برای پرداخت تمام بدهی‌ها و میراث نشانگر نیز در صورت ناکافی بودن صندوق مشخص شده برای پوشش آن کم می‌شود. برای مثال، املاک A می‌تواند شامل یک نقاشی، 300 پوند در حساب ذخیره و 700 پوند در پول دیگر باشد. بدهی‌های 100 پوند وجود دارد اما وصیت‌نامه A نقاشی را به B، 500 پوند از حساب ذخیره به C، 800 پوند به D و 200 پوند به E می‌گرداند. B نقاشی را دریافت می‌کند. میراث نشانگر C به 300 پوند کاهش می‌یابد و پس از پرداخت بدهی‌ها از 700 پوند باقی مانده، میراث‌های عمومی D و E در نسبت کاهش می‌یابند، به 480 پوند و 120 پوند. وقتی انوئیتی‌های در وصیت‌نامه داده می‌شوند، قانون عمومی این است که آنها در تاریخ مرگ معلم ارزیابی می‌شوند، سپس در مطابقت با این ارزیابی در نسبت کاهش می‌یابند و هر انوئیتی مقدار کاهش یافته را دریافت می‌کند. تمام این قوانین در صورت بیان هرگونه قصد برعکس در وصیت‌نامه، اعمال می‌شوند. 3. \"_ Place the Farsi and English text line by line from one point to the first point next to each other in such a way that one line of English text is followed by two empty lines, followed by the Persian translation and continue this process until the end of the text.A\\nabandonment n. 1. The act of giving up a legal right, particularly a right of ownership of property. Property that has been abandoned is res nullius (a thing belonging to no one), and a person taking possession of it therefore acquires a lawful title. An item is regarded as abandoned when it can be established that the original owner has discarded it and is indifferent as to what becomes of it: such an item cannot be the subject of a theft charge. However, property placed by its owner in a dustbin is not abandoned, having been placed there for the purpose of being collected as refuse. In marine insurance, abandonment is the surrender of all rights to a ship or cargo in a case of constructive total loss. The insured person must do this by giving the insurer within a reasonable time a notice of abandonment, by which he relinquishes all his rights to the ship or cargo to the insurer and can treat the loss as if it were an actual total loss. 2. In civil litigation, the relinquishing of the whole or part of the claim made in an action or of an appeal. Any claim is now considered to be abandoned once a *notice of discontinuance is served, according to rule 38 (1) of the *Civil Procedure Rules. 3. The offence of a parent or guardian. leaving a child under the age of 16 to its fate. A child is not regarded as abandoned if the parent knows and approves steps someone else is taking to look after it. The court may allow a child to be adopted without the consent of its parents if they are guilty of abandonment.\\nabatement n. 1. (of debts) The proportionate reduction in the payment of debts that takes place if a person's assets are insufficient to settle with his creditors in full. 2. (of legacies) The reduction or cancellation of legacies when the estate is insufficient to cover all the legacies provided for in the will or on intestacy after payment of the deceased's debts. The Administration of Estates Act 1925 provides that general legacies, unless given to satisfy a debt or for other consideration, abate in proportion to the amounts of those legacies; specific and demonstrative legacies then abate if the estate is still insufficient to pay all debts, and a demonstrative legacy also abates if the specified fund is insufficient to cover it. For example, A's estate may comprise a painting, £300 in his savings account, and £700 in other money; there are debts of £100 but his will leaves the painting to B, £500 from the savings account to C. £800 to D, and £200 to E. B will receive the painting. C's demonstrative legacy abates to £300, and after the debts are paid from the remaining £700, D's and E's general legacies abate proportionately, to £480 and £120 respectively. When annuities are given by the will, the general rule is that they are valued the date of the testator's death, then abate proportionately in accordance with that valuation, and each annuitant receives the abated sum. All these rules are subject to any contrary intention being expressed in the will. 3. (in land law) Any reduction or cancellation of money payable. For example a lease may provide for an abatement of rent in certain circumstances, e.g. if the building is destroyed by fire, and a purchaser of land may claim an abatement of the price if the seller can prove his ownership of only part of the land he contracted to sell. 4. (of nuisances) The termination, removal, or destruction of a *nuisance. A person injured by a nuisance has a right to abate it. In doing so, he must not do more damage than is necessary and, if removal of the nuisance requires entry on to the property from which it emanates, he may have to give notice to the wrongdoer. (of debts) The proportionate reduction in the payment of debts that takes place if a person's assets are insufficient to settle with his creditors in full. 2. (of legacies) The reduction or cancellation of legacies when the estate is insufficient to cover all the legacies provided for in the will or on intestacy after payment of the deceased's debts. The Administration of Estates Act 1925 provides that general legacies, unless given to satisfy a debt or for other consideration, abate in proportion to the amounts of those legacies; specific and demonstrative legacies then abate if the estate is still insufficient to pay all debts, and a demonstrative legacy also abates if the specified fund is insufficient to cover it. For example, A's estate may comprise a painting, £300 in his savings account, and £700 in other money; there are debts of £100 but his will leaves the painting to B, £500 from the savings account to C, £800 to D, and £200 to E. B will receive the painting. C's demonstrative legacy abates to £300, and after the debts are paid from the remaining £700, D's and E's general legacies abate proportionately, to £480 and £120 respectively. When annuities are given by the will, the general rule is that they are valued the date of the testator's death, then abate proportionately in accordance with that valuation, and each annuitant receives the abated sum. All these rules are subject to any contrary intention being expressed in the will. 3. (in land law) Any reduction or cancellation of money payable. For example a lease may provide for an abatement of rent in certain circumstances, e.g. if the building is destroyed by fire, and a purchaser of land may claim an abatement of the price if the seller can prove his ownership of only part of the land he contracted to sell. 4. In doing so, he must not do more damage than is necessary and, if removal of the nuisance requires entry on to the property from which it emanates, he may have to give notice to the wrongdoer. (of proceedings) The\\n\\n**Farsi**\\n\\n**تندکنی**\\n1. حق قانونی خاصی، به‌ویژه حق مالکیت بر اموال را رها کردن. اموالی که رها شده‌اند، متعلق به کسی نیستند (res nullius) و کسی که آنها را در اختیار می‌گیرد، عنوان قانونی را کسب می‌کند. یک مورد را رها شده می‌دانند當 زمانی که ثابت شود که مالک اصلی آن را رها کرده و بی‌توجه به آنچه با آن می‌شود. اما اموالی که توسط مالکشان در سطل زباله قرار می‌گیرند، رها نشده‌اند، زیرا برای جمع‌آوری به عنوان زباله قرار داده شده‌اند. در بیمه دریایی، رها کردن تمام حقوقی که در مورد کشتی یا بار در مورد تلفات کل ساختاری وجود دارد. شخص بیمه شده باید این کار را با ارسال اعلان رها کردن به بیمه‌گر در زمان معقول انجام دهد، که در آن تمام حقوقش را به بیمه‌گر رها می‌کند و می‌تواند از تلفات به عنوان یک تلفات کل واقعی برخوردار شود. 2. در دعاوی مدنی، رها کردن کل یا بخشی از مدعی در یک اقدام یا در یک استدلال. هر مدعی اکنون پس از ارسال اعلان توقف به‌جای می‌گردد، بر اساس قانون 38 (1) از قوانین روند مدنی. 3. جرم پدر یا سرپرست. کودکی زیر 16 سال را رها کرد. کودکی را نمی‌دانند رها شده‌است اگر پدر بدانید و تأیید اقدامات دیگری را برای مراقبت از آن انجام دهد. دادگاه می‌تواند اجازه دهد کودکی را بدون رضایت والدین به فرزندخواندگی بپذیرد اگر آنها از رها کردن گناهکار باشند.\\n\\n**کمک**\\n1. (در مورد بدهی‌ها) کاهش نسبت‌دار در پرداخت بدهی‌ها که در صورت ناکافی بودن دارایی‌های شخص برای تسویه با اعضایش در کل رخ می‌دهد. 2. (در مورد میراث‌ها) کاهش یا لغو میراث‌ها當 زمانی که املاک برای پوشش تمام میراث‌هایی که در وصیت‌نامه یا بر اساس قانون مورث‌گردان پس از پرداخت بدهی‌های مردگان وجود دارد، ناکافی باشد. قانون مدیریت املاک 1925 می‌گوید که میراث‌های عمومی، مگر اینکه برای پرداخت بدهی یا برای سایر در نظر گرفتن داده شده باشند، در نسبت به مقدار آنها کم می‌شوند. میراث‌های خاص و نشانگر در صورت ناکافی بودن املاک برای پرداخت تمام بدهی‌ها و میراث نشانگر نیز در صورت ناکافی بودن صندوق مشخص شده برای پوشش آن کم می‌شود. برای مثال، املاک A می‌تواند شامل یک نقاشی، 300 پوند در حساب ذخیره و 700 پوند در پول دیگر باشد. بدهی‌های 100 پوند وجود دارد اما وصیت‌نامه A نقاشی را به B، 500 پوند از حساب ذخیره به C، 800 پوند به D و 200 پوند به E می‌گرداند. B نقاشی را دریافت می‌کند. میراث نشانگر C به 300 پوند کاهش می‌یابد و پس از پرداخت بدهی‌ها از 700 پوند باقی مانده، میراث‌های عمومی D و E در نسبت کاهش می‌یابند، به 480 پوند و 120 پوند. وقتی انوئیتی‌های در وصیت‌نامه داده می‌شوند، قانون عمومی این است که آنها در تاریخ مرگ معلم ارزیابی می‌شوند، سپس در مطابقت با این ارزیابی در نسبت کاهش می‌یابند و هر انوئیتی مقدار کاهش یافته را دریافت می‌کند. تمام این قوانین در صورت بیان هرگونه قصد برعکس در وصیت‌نامه، اعمال می‌شوند. 3. (در قانون زمین) هر کاهش یا لغو پول قابل پرداخت. برای مثال، اجاره ممکن است در شرایط خاصی برای کاهش اجاره در نظر گرفته شود، مانند اگر ساختمان توسط آتش نابود شود و خریدار زمین می‌تواند برای کاهش قیمت ادعای کند اگر فروشنده بتواند مالکیت خود را بر بخشی از زمین که در قرارداد فروخته شده‌است، ثابت کند. 4. (در مورد مزاحمات) پایان، برداشتن یا نابود کردن مزاحمات. کسی که توسط مزاحمات آسیب دیده‌است، حق دارد آنها را کم کند. در انجام این کار، باید آسیب بیشتری ایجاد نکند و اگر برداشتن مزاحمات وارد شدن به املاک از آن سرچشمه می‌گیرد، باید به متخلف اعلان دهد. یک شورا می‌تواند اعلان کم کردن برای کنترل مزاحمات قانونی را صادر کند. 5.
